A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

美国每周流感监测报告:2026年9月19日结束的第37周关键更新 | FluView - 美国疾病控制与预防中心 | CDC (.gov)

Weekly US Influenza Surveillance Report: Key Updates for Week 37, ending September 19, 2026 | FluView - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)于2026年10月2日发布了2026年第37周(截至9月19日)的流感监测周报。报告指出流感活动水平较低,临床实验室检测的呼吸道标本中有2.7%呈流感病毒阳性。其中,甲型流感病毒占96.1%,乙型流感病毒占3.9%。未报告新的新型流感病毒人类感染病例。CDC还指出,变异流感病毒(如H5N1)尚未表现出在人与人之间容易且可持续传播的能力。报告包含公共卫生实验室的数据,这些实验室监测不同型别和谱系的流行病毒比例。报告强调,仅在CDC进行检测的标本不包含在内,且可能包含每人多个标本。CDC提供了针对禽流感A(H5)病毒的临时预防建议和公共卫生调查指南。
查看新闻五要素
时间
第37周,截至2026年9月19日;发布于2026年10月2日。
地点与对象
美国;一般人群。
事件
CDC发布了2026年第37周(截至9月19日)的流感监测周报,显示流感活动水平较低。报告包含来自临床和公共卫生实验室的病毒学监测数据。
规模、影响与风险
在临床实验室中,检测的呼吸道标本中有2.7%呈流感病毒阳性。其中,甲型流感病毒占96.1%,乙型流感病毒占3.9%。未报告新的新型流感病毒人类感染病例。报告指出,变异流感病毒(如H5N1)尚未表现出在人与人之间容易且可持续传播的能力。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC通过病毒学监测监测流感活动情况,并提供针对预防和公共卫生调查的临时建议。报告未包含仅在CDC检测的标本数据,且可能包含每人多个标本。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Weekly US Influenza Surveillance Report: Key Updates for Week 37, ending September 19, 2026 | FluView - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) released its weekly influenza surveillance report for Week 37, ending September 19, 2026, on October 2, 2026. The report highlights low seasonal influenza activity, with 2.7% of respiratory specimens tested in clinical laboratories showing positive results for influenza virus. Influenza A accounted for 96.1% of positive specimens, while Influenza B accounted for 3.9%. No new confirmed human infections with a novel influenza virus were reported. The CDC also notes that variant influenza viruses, such as H5N1, have not shown the ability to spread easily and sustainably from person to person. The report includes data from public health laboratories, which monitor the proportion of circulating viruses by type and lineage. It emphasizes that specimens tested only at CDC are not included, and multiple specimens per person may be reported. The CDC provides interim recommendations for prevention and public health investigations related to avian influenza A(H5) viruses.
Original source text (English)

The results of tests performed by clinical laboratories nationwide are summarized below. Data from clinical laboratories (the percentage of specimens tested that are positive for influenza) are used to monitor whether influenza activity is increasing or decreasing. Most human infections with variant influenza viruses occur following exposure to swine, but person-to-person transmission can occur. It is important to note that in most cases, variant influenza viruses have not shown the ability to spread easily and sustainably from person to person. Additional information regarding human infections with novel influenza A viruses: Surveillance Methods | FluView Interactive Outpatient and Emergency Department Illness Surveillance Outpatient Respiratory Illness Visits The U.S. Outpatient Influenza-like Illness Surveillance Network (ILINet) monitors outpatient visits for respiratory illness referred to as influenza-like illness [ILI (fever plus cough or sore throat)], not laboratory-confirmed influenza, and may capture respiratory illness visits due to infection with any pathogen that can present with similar symptoms, including influenza, SARS-CoV-2, and RSV. Therefore, it is important to evaluate syndromic surveillance data, including that from ILINet, in the context of other sources of surveillance data to obtain a complete and accurate picture of influenza, SARS-CoV-2, and other respiratory virus activity. Nationwide during Week 37, 1.6% of patient visits reported through ILINet were due to respiratory illness that included fever plus a cough or sore throat, also referred to as ILI. Multiple respiratory viruses are co-circulating, and the relative contribution of influenza virus infection to ILI varies by location. View Chart Data Outpatient Respiratory Illness Visits by Age Group More than 70% of ILINet participants provide both the number of patient visits for respiratory illness and the total number of patient visits for the week broken out by age group. Data from this subset of providers are used to calculate the percentages of patient visits for respiratory illness by age group. During Week 37, the percentage of visits for respiratory illness reported in ILINet was 5.2% among those 0-4 years, 2.3% among those 5-24 years, 1.4% among those 25-49 years, 1.0% among those 50-64 years, and 0.7% among those 65 years and older. View Larger NSSP week 37 Additional information about emergency department visits for flu for current and past seasons: Surveillance Methods | Emergency Department Visits for COVID-19, flu, and RSV Hospitalization surveillance FluSurv-Net Influenza-Associated Hospitalizations: The Influenza Hospitalization Surveillance Network (FluSurv-NET) conducts population-based surveillance for laboratory-confirmed influenza-related hospitalizations in select counties in 14 states and represents approximately 10% of the U.S. population. FluSurv-NET hospitalization data are preliminary. As data are received each week, prior case counts and rates are updated accordingly. A total of 31,363 laboratory-confirmed influenza-associated hospitalizations were reported by FluSurv-NET sites between October 1, 2025, and September 19, 2026. The weekly hospitalization rate observed during Week 37 was 0.3 per 100,000 population. The cumulative hospitalization rate observed in Week 37 was 89.6 per 100,000 population. Additional FluSurv-NET data are available on FluView Interactive including hospitalization rates for the current and past seasons by age, sex, and race/ethnicity (http://gis.cdc.gov/GRASP/Fluview/FluHospRates.html) as well as data on patient characteristics at: (http://gis.cdc.gov/grasp/fluview/FluHospChars.html.) FluSurv-NET data are used to generate national estimates of the total numbers of influenza cases, medical visits, hospitalizations and deaths. This season, CDC is reporting preliminary cumulative in-season estimates, which are available at Estimated US Flu Disease Burden | Flu Burden | CDC. The CDC released a weekly influenza surveillance report for Week 37, ending September 19, 2026, indicating low seasonal influenza activity. The report includes virologic surveillance data from clinical and public health laboratories.

In clinical laboratories, 2.7% of respiratory specimens tested were positive for influenza virus. Influenza A accounted for 96.1% of positive specimens, while Influenza B accounted for 3.9%. The report notes that variant influenza viruses, such as H5N1, have not shown the ability to spread easily and sustainably from person to person.

The CDC is monitoring influenza activity through virologic surveillance and provides interim recommendations for prevention and public health investigations. The report does not include data on specimens tested only at CDC and may include multiple specimens per person.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

儿童在季节变化期间更容易感染RSV和季节性流感:警惕呼吸窘迫迹象。- Vietnam.vn

Children are more susceptible to RSV and seasonal flu during seasonal changes: Be alert for signs of respiratory distress. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南vn于2026年10月4日发表的文章指出,儿童在季节变化期间对呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)和季节性流感的易感性增加。文章强调了识别呼吸窘迫早期预警迹象的重要性,例如发热、咳嗽和流鼻涕,这些症状可能发展为严重的疾病,如支气管炎、肺炎或呼吸衰竭。一名来自河内的19个月大的儿童因持续发热、咳嗽和呼吸困难而被诊断为RSV引起的急性支气管炎和急性鼻咽炎。该儿童的症状包括呼吸急促、胸骨凹陷和喘息,这些症状通过快速呼吸道检测和胸部X光确认。该病例强调了家长需要监测儿童的呼吸频率和其他临床迹象,因为这些迹象可能表明疾病的严重程度。Medlatec Tay Ho诊所的医生开具了门诊治疗并安排了随访。文章作为公共卫生提醒,强调了RSV和季节性流感对婴幼儿的风险,尤其是2岁以下儿童、早产儿以及有基础疾病的孩子。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章于2026-10-04发表。事件日期未明确提及,但症状在2026-10-01就诊前3天开始报告。
地点与对象
该病例涉及一名来自越南河内的19个月大的儿童。文章还讨论了2岁以下儿童、早产儿以及有基础疾病儿童的一般风险。
事件
一名来自河内的19个月大的儿童被诊断为RSV引起的急性支气管炎和急性鼻咽炎。该儿童表现出持续发热、咳嗽和呼吸困难,症状发展为喘息、呼吸急促、胸骨凹陷和其他症状。
规模、影响与风险
该病例突显了RSV和治疗在婴幼儿中可能导致严重并发症,如支气管炎、肺炎或呼吸衰竭的潜在风险。该儿童被开具了门诊治疗并安排了随访。
应对、状态与不确定性
该儿童在Medlatec Tay Ho诊所接受治疗并接受了门诊护理。诊断通过快速呼吸道检测和胸部X光确认。该病例是关于儿童中RSV和季节性流感风险的更广泛公共卫生意识宣传活动的一部分。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Children are more susceptible to RSV and seasonal flu during seasonal changes: Be alert for signs of respiratory distress. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The article published on October 4, 2026, by Vietnam.vn highlights the increased susceptibility of children to respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and seasonal flu during seasonal changes. It emphasizes the importance of recognizing early warning signs of respiratory distress, such as fever, cough, and runny nose, which can progress to severe conditions like bronchiolitis, pneumonia, or respiratory failure. A 19-month-old child from Hanoi was diagnosed with acute bronchiolitis due to RSV and acute nasopharyngitis after presenting with persistent fever, cough, and difficulty breathing. The child's symptoms included rapid breathing, chest retraction, and wheezing, which were confirmed through a rapid respiratory test and chest X-ray. The case underscores the need for parents to monitor their children's breathing rate and other clinical signs, as these can indicate the severity of the illness. Doctors at Medlatec Tay Ho Polyclinic prescribed outpatient treatment and scheduled a follow-up. The article serves as a public health reminder about the risks of RSV and seasonal flu in young children, especially those under two years old, premature babies, and those with underlying medical conditions.
Original source text (English)
Children are more susceptible to RSV and seasonal flu during seasonal changes: Be alert for signs of respiratory distress. The changing seasons are a time when respiratory illnesses in children are at increased risk, with respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and seasonal influenza potentially causing complications such as pneumonia and respiratory failure. Starting with symptoms similar to a common cold, the illness can progress rapidly, especially in infants, children under 2 years old, premature babies, and children with underlying medical conditions. Báo Nhân dân • 04/10/2026 (Illustrative image: BVCC) From cold-like symptoms to the risk of respiratory failure. Doctors at Medlatec Tay Ho Polyclinic recently received a 19-month-old child, NPN, from Hanoi , who presented with a persistent fever, cough, and difficulty breathing. The child started having a fever 3 days before the visit, with a peak temperature of 39°C, and about 3 episodes per day. After taking fever-reducing medication, the condition improved but did not completely resolve, accompanied by a persistent cough and runny nose. The following day, the child coughed more, producing thick, heavy phlegm, vomited after coughing, had thick white nasal discharge, and had loose stools 4-5 times a day. The family gave the child fever reducers and cough syrup at home, but the symptoms did not improve, so they took the child to the doctor. Examination results showed the child had a heart rate of 150 beats/minute, a respiratory rate of 60 breaths/minute, and mild chest retraction. The lungs had numerous wheezing and rhonchi on both sides. A rapid respiratory test for 5 respiratory pathogens yielded positive results; chest X-ray revealed thickening of the bronchial walls in both lungs. Ear, nose, and throat endoscopy revealed mucous accumulation in the palate and redness and swelling of the pharyngeal mucosa. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA The child was diagnosed with acute bronchiolitis due to respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and acute nasopharyngitis, prescribed outpatient treatment, and scheduled for a follow-up appointment. This case shows that initial symptoms such as fever, cough, and runny nose in young children should not be taken lightly, especially when additional signs such as rapid breathing, chest retraction, or wheezing appear. Chest X-ray images revealed thickening of the bronchial walls in both lungs. According to Dr. Nguyen Thanh Nam, Director of the Pediatric Center at Bach Mai Hospital, the respiratory system of young children is not yet fully developed. The airways, especially in newborns and young children, are narrow, so even slight swelling, increased mucus secretion, or airway constriction can significantly affect airflow. When infected with respiratory viruses such as RSV or influenza, some children may progress from relatively mild initial symptoms to bronchiolitis, pneumonia, or respiratory failure. Acute bronchiolitis is a lower respiratory tract infection that causes inflammation and obstruction of the small bronchioles, commonly seen in children under 24 months of age, especially those under 12 months. RSV is one of the leading causes, accounting for approximately 70-80% of bronchiolitis cases. When RSV causes inflammation of the bronchioles, the small airways can become swollen, produce more mucus, and narrow. As a result, children may experience wheezing, rapid breathing, chest retraction, and difficulty feeding. If the disease progresses severely, children may develop pneumonia and respiratory failure, requiring intensive hospitalization and respiratory support. You may also like Meanwhile, seasonal influenza typically starts suddenly with fever, cough, sore throat, headache, body aches, and fatigue. In young children, it can also cause vomiting, diarrhea, loss of appetite, or refusal to feed. Despite their different manifestations, both RSV and seasonal influenza have the potential to cause serious complications. Influenza can even cause extrapulmonary complications in some cases. Therefore, parents should not rely solely on fever to assess their child's condition. Recognize the warning signs; don't wait until your child has a high fever. According to experts, RSV infection symptoms often begin like a common cold with cough, runny nose, nasal congestion, sneezing, and mild or high fever. Later, the illness may progress with increased coughing, wheezing, rapid breathing, and difficulty breathing. One of the important signs parents need to observe is the breathing rate and the level of effort required when the child breathes. When a child has lower respiratory tract injury, the body may respond by increasing its breathing rate. Parents can observe the child's chest or abdomen and count the number of breaths per minute, ideally when the child is calm or asleep. In particular, pay attention to signs of chest retraction. This is a condition where, with each inhalation, the area below the ribs or between the ribs sinks noticeably inward, indicating that the child is having to increase the effort of breathing and is at risk of respiratory failure. Symptoms such as nasal flaring, grunting, nodding in rhythm with breathing, wheezing, or faster-than-normal breathing should also be noted. In infants, symptoms are sometimes atypical, and may include poor feeding, lethargy, or even hypothermia in severe infections. According to experts at Bach Mai Hospital, parents should take their children to a medical facility immediately if the child breathes rapidly, with difficulty breathing, experiences chest retraction, nasal flaring, or groans while breathing. Bluish discoloration of the lips, tongue, or skin, lethargy, difficulty waking up, poor responsiveness, or unusual irritability are also dangerous signs. In particular, if a child refuses to breastfeed, is unable to drink, vomits frequently, or shows signs of dehydration, the family should take the child to the doctor immediately. Cases involving apnea, intermittent breathing, rapidly worsening difficulty breathing, seizures, or fever accompanied by abnormal general condition require prompt emergency care. Parents need to be especially cautious with newborns, premature babies, or children with underlying heart or lung conditions because the disease can progress rapidly. Experts also note that parents should not assess the severity of the illness based on a single sign or indicator. Doctors need to combine respiratory rate, blood oxygen status, level of alertness, ability to eat, and other clinical manifestations to make treatment decisions. Given the increased risk of respiratory illnesses during seasonal changes, proactive prevention is a crucial solution to reduce the risk of illness and complications in children. You may also like For seasonal influenza, children 6 months of age and older can receive the recommended flu vaccine. Regular booster shots are necessary because the influenza virus mutates over time and the vaccine's protective effect gradually decreases. For RSV, there are currently several immunization measures available for certain groups, depending on recommendations and accessibility in each country and healthcare facility. These measures include RSV vaccination for pregnant women at the appropriate time and prophylactic monoclonal antibodies for children in the indicated groups. Parents should consult with their doctor to choose the appropriate measure based on the child's age, health status, and risk factors. In addition, it is necessary to maintain the habit of washing hands frequently, limit children's contact with people who have respiratory illnesses, keep the living environment well-ventilated, and absolutely prevent children from being exposed to cigarette smoke. In particular, parents should not buy antibiotics or use medication without a prescription. Antibiotics are not effective in treating viral illnesses such as RSV and influenza; they should only be used when there is evidence or suspicion of a bacterial infection requiring treatment as assessed by a doctor. When children exhibit symptoms such as coughing, fever, wheezing, rapid breathing, or poor feeding, families should take them to a doctor for assessment and appropriate treatment guidance, and not wait until obvious breathing difficulties appear before seeking medical help. 季节性流感 在老年人、幼儿和孕妇中可能较为严重。- Vietnam.vn

Seasonal influenza can be severe in the elderly, young children, and pregnant women. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南新闻网站 Vietnam.vn 于 2026 年 10 月 3 日发布的新闻报道指出,季节性流感 在易感群体如老年人、幼儿和孕妇中可能较为严重。卫生部疾病预防部门指出,这些群体以及患有基础疾病或免疫系统较弱的人群,更容易出现严重症状。报道强调,该疾病主要通过呼吸道飞沫或接触被污染的表面传播,并且在过渡季节时达到高峰。尽管 季节性流感 的病例略有增加,但尚未记录到严重病例或死亡的异常上升。疾病预防部门建议采取积极措施,如在拥挤场所佩戴口罩、保持个人卫生以及避免与患有呼吸道疾病的人密切接触。这些建议旨在保护公众健康并减少病毒传播。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal influenza can be severe in the elderly, young children, and pregnant women. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The news report from Vietnam.vn, published on October 3, 2026, highlights that seasonal influenza can be severe in vulnerable groups such as the elderly, young children, and pregnant women. The Department of Disease Prevention, Ministry of Health, notes that these groups, along with individuals with underlying diseases or weakened immune systems, are at higher risk of developing severe symptoms. The report emphasizes that the disease is mainly transmitted through respiratory droplets or contact with contaminated surfaces and tends to peak during transitional seasons. While there has been a slight increase in seasonal influenza cases, no unusual rise in severe cases or deaths has been recorded. The Department of Disease Prevention recommends proactive measures such as wearing masks in crowded places, maintaining personal hygiene, and avoiding close contact with those who have respiratory illnesses. These recommendations aim to protect public health and reduce the spread of the virus.
Original source text (English)
Seasonal influenza can be severe in the elderly, young children, and pregnant women. (Inspection) - The Department of Disease Prevention, Ministry of Health, notes that people over 65 years old, children under 5 years old, pregnant women, especially in the last three months of pregnancy, or those who have recently given birth (within 2 weeks), people with underlying diseases and weakened immune systems are at risk of developing severe symptoms when infected with influenza. Báo Thanh tra • 03/10/2026 According to the Department of Disease Prevention, seasonal influenza is an acute respiratory infection caused by the influenza virus. The disease is mainly transmitted through the respiratory tract, via respiratory droplets or contact with surfaces contaminated with the virus. The disease occurs year-round but tends to peak during transitional seasons, especially when the weather alternates erratically between hot and cold spells. According to the Department of Disease Control, in recent weeks, the number of seasonal influenza cases has shown a slight increase. However, no unusual increase in severe cases or deaths due to influenza has been recorded. Most people with the flu experience mild symptoms and recover on their own. However, the disease can become severe and cause complications, especially in the elderly, young children, pregnant women, and people with underlying health conditions or weakened immune systems. Khám phá thêm Điểm đến du lịch Du lịch Du lịch và vận tải To proactively protect the health of yourself, your family, and the community, the Department of Disease Prevention recommends that people wear masks correctly when visiting medical facilities, on public transport, in crowded places, or in enclosed spaces with poor ventilation. People with respiratory symptoms should wear masks when in contact with others. People should cover their mouths and noses when coughing or sneezing with a disposable tissue or their elbow; dispose of used tissues properly and wash their hands immediately afterward. They should also wash their hands frequently with soap and water or hand sanitizer, and avoid touching their eyes, nose, and mouth. The Department of Disease Prevention also recommends maintaining personal hygiene; regularly cleaning frequently touched surfaces; ensuring good ventilation in homes, workplaces, and classrooms; and increasing ventilation in enclosed spaces. People should limit close contact with those who have or are suspected of having respiratory illnesses. Those with symptoms such as fever, cough, sore throat, and runny nose should limit contact with others, rest, and monitor their health. You may also like Hanoi proactively adapts to the "silver wave". The aging population places increasing demands on policies for the elderly, not only in terms of social security but also in terms of their spiritual well-being and creating conditions for them to continue contributing to society. To make population aging more than just a social security issue. A sustainable social security policy goes beyond simply ensuring a decent life and healthcare for the elderly; it also needs to create spaces for them to continue learning, socializing, working, contributing, sharing experiences, and developing their intellect. Elderly people and those with serious illnesses will receive free regular health check-ups at home. The Ministry of Health allows localities to organize free home-based routine health checkups for the elderly, seriously ill patients, bedridden individuals, or those with difficulty traveling to examination facilities. In addition, it is necessary to maintain a healthy lifestyle, eat a balanced diet, exercise appropriately, and get enough sleep. People with underlying conditions need to adhere to their treatment and manage their conditions well. The Department of Disease Prevention advises people not to self-medicate with antiviral drugs or antibiotics without a prescription from a healthcare professional. According to the Department of Disease Prevention, groups at risk of developing severe symptoms from influenza include people over 65 years old; children under 5 years old; pregnant women, especially in the last three months of pregnancy, or women who have given birth within the last two weeks; people with underlying conditions such as cardiovascular disease, chronic lung disease, kidney failure, cirrhosis, diabetes; and people with weakened immune systems. Khám phá thêm Tiểu bang & Chính quyền Địa phương Sở thích & Thời gian rỗi Tham khảo Địa lý Individuals in the above-mentioned risk groups or those showing signs of worsening illness should contact or visit a healthcare facility for timely advice, examination, and treatment. The Department of Disease Prevention, Ministry of Health, stated that it will continue to closely coordinate with relevant units to closely monitor the developments of seasonal influenza; update information promptly; provide professional guidance and implement appropriate prevention and control measures. During September and the first nine months of 2026, the Ministry of Health continued to monitor the situation regarding several infectious diseases and food poisoning incidents, recording the number of cases and deaths across various disease groups. According to a report from the Ministry of Health, from August 18th to September 17th, the whole country recorded 15,928 cases of dengue fever (1 death); 6,854 cases of hand, foot and mouth disease; 138 cases of measles-like rash; 32 cases of viral encephalitis; one case of meningococcal meningitis; and 8 deaths due to rabies. As of September 17th, the total number of people living with HIV nationwide is nearly 257,800, and the number of deaths due to HIV/AIDS nationwide is 120,400. Regarding food poisoning, in September there were 5 incidents affecting 382 people (2 deaths). Overall, in the first 9 months of this year, the whole country experienced 62 food poisoning incidents affecting 3,137 people (9 deaths). B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

香港一例严重儿童流感病例:疫苗接种呼吁保护健康

Severe Pediatric Influenza Case in Hong Kong: Vaccination Urged to Protect Health
新闻简报2026年10月5日,香港报告了一例严重儿童流感病例,一名10岁男孩于10月2日出现症状,并于10月4日被送入儿科重症监护病房。卫生防护中心(CHP)确认该患者感染了甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09,并未接种2026/27 季节性流感 疫苗接种(SIV)。卫生防护中心强调了疫苗接种和卫生措施的重要性,以预防流感传播,尤其是在学校中,自9月以来已记录近350例暴发,影响超过2000人。患者目前仍住院,病情严重,卫生防护中心正在继续调查。自2026年9月17日启动的SIV推广计划旨在于12月中旬完成所有活动,同时引入了新的电子同意书以简化流程。卫生防护中心呼吁学校和家长合作,确保儿童尽早接种疫苗以降低风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该病例于2026年10月5日被报告,患者于同一天被送入儿科重症监护病房。事件日期为2026年10月2日,患者出现症状。香港流感季始于2026年6月下旬,最新监测数据显示,截至2026年10月5日,流感活动水平较高。
地点与对象
该病例发生在香港,具体涉及一名10岁男孩。受影响的人群包括儿童、老年人以及患有基础疾病或免疫系统受损的人群,因为这些群体被列为高风险人群。
事件
香港卫生防护中心(CHP)记录了一例严重的儿童流感感染病例。患者于2026年10月2日出现发热、咳嗽、流鼻涕和头晕症状,并于2026年10月4日被送入儿科重症监护病房。卫生防护中心还表示,患者在潜伏期内没有旅行史,并未接种2026/27 季节性流感 疫苗接种(SIV)。
规模、影响与风险
自2026年6月底以来,香港已记录22例严重儿童流感感染病例。其中一名患者已去世,两名患者病情严重,两名患者病情危重。卫生防护中心指出,由于社区流感传播的广泛传播,感染病例和严重病例均显著增加。此外,自9月以来,学校中已记录近350例流感样疾病暴发,影响超过2000人。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生防护中心正在对该病例进行初步调查,并将继续进行。患者目前仍住院,病情严重。卫生防护中心已启动2026/27 SIV计划,包括SIV学校推广计划(SIVSOP),该计划于2026年9月17日开始。预计推广活动将在12月中旬完成,比去年提前约两周。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Severe Pediatric Influenza Case in Hong Kong: Vaccination Urged to Protect Health

News Brief
A severe pediatric influenza case was reported in Hong Kong on October 5, 2026, involving a 10-year-old boy who developed symptoms on October 2 and was admitted to the pediatric intensive care unit on October 4. The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) confirmed the patient tested positive for influenza A (H1) virus and had not received the 2026/27 Seasonal Influenza Vaccination (SIV). The CHP emphasized the importance of vaccination and hygiene measures to prevent influenza transmission, especially in schools where nearly 350 outbreaks were recorded since September, affecting over 2,000 individuals. The patient remains hospitalized in serious condition, and the CHP is continuing its investigation. The SIV outreach program, which started on September 17, aims to complete activities by mid-December, with a new electronic consent form introduced to streamline the process. The CHP urged schools and parents to collaborate and ensure children receive the vaccine as early as possible to mitigate risks.
Original source text (English)
Skip to Content Javascript must be enabled for the correct page display Severe Pediatric Influenza Case in Hong Kong: Vaccination Urged to Protect Health News China Business TECH ENT Sport HK HotTV Asia Pacific Blog HK News China Business TECH ENT Sport HK HotTV Asia Pacific Blog Font Size A A Home HotNews NEWS CHINA BUSINESS TECH ENT SPORT HK ASIA PACIFIC BLOG HK HK Severe Pediatric Influenza Case in Hong Kong: Vaccination Urged to Protect Health 2026-10-05 19:13 Last Updated At:19:28 Facebook Weibo CHP records case of severe paediatric influenza infection The Centre for Health Protection (CHP) of the Department of Health today (October 5) recorded a severe paediatric influenza infection case. The CHP urged the public to receive vaccinations as early as possible to safeguard their own health and that of their families. In addition, the public should maintain good personal and environmental hygiene at all times to prevent influenza and other respiratory illnesses. The case involves a 10-year-old boy with good past health. He developed a fever, cough, runny nose and dizziness on October 2. He was brought to the Accident and Emergency Department of Prince of Wales Hospital on October 4. He was admitted to the paediatric intensive care unit for treatment on the same day. His respiratory specimen tested positive for influenza A (H1) virus. His clinical diagnosis was influenza A infection complicated with shock. The patient remains hospitalised and is in serious condition. The CHP's preliminary investigation revealed that the patient had no travel history during the incubation period, and had not received the 2026/27 Seasonal Influenza Vaccination (SIV). One of his household contacts recently developed a mild fever and upper respiratory symptoms, but the symptoms were mild and the household contact concerned has recovered. The CHP will continue to investigate the case. Hong Kong entered the influenza season in late June. The latest surveillance data showed that influenza activity remains at a high level. Since the end of June, a cumulative total of 22 cases of severe paediatric influenza infection have been recorded. Among these cases, 16 patients have recovered and been discharged from hospital, two patients are in stable condition, two are in serious condition, one is in critical condition and one has passed away. Due to widespread influenza transmission in the community in recent months, there has been a significant increase in both the number of infections and severe cases. Since the new school year started in September, nearly 350 influenza-like illness outbreaks have been recorded in schools, affecting over 2 000 persons. Therefore, schools and parents must remain vigilant and take preventive measures. The 2026/27 SIV Programmes, including the SIV School Outreach Programme (SIVSOP), commenced on September 17. The SIVSOP this year has introduced a new Electronic Consent Form (e-consent form) feature via eHealth. This new measure not only facilitates the public to join the SIV Programmes but also streamlines the administrative procedures for healthcare service providers and schools, so that SIV outreach activities can start as early as possible. Approximately 100 schools have organised SIV outreach activities, a significant increase compared to the same period last year. SIV outreach activities are expected to be completed by mid-December, about two weeks earlier than last year. The CHP appeals to all schools and parents in Hong Kong to work closely with the CHP and complete the consent process as soon as possible so that students can be arranged to receive SIV earlier. The CHP reminds parents that the health condition of children with influenza can deteriorate rapidly. Parents must pay close attention to their condition, and visit an accident and emergency department immediately if the child's condition deteriorates with symptoms such as shortness of breath, wheezing, blue lips, chest pain, confusion, a persistent fever or convulsions. Members of the public should seek medical attention as soon as possible if they develop respiratory symptoms. If they live with members of high-risk groups, such as children, the elderly and those with underlying illnesses or compromised immune systems, they should wear a surgical mask at home to reduce the risk of infecting them. Other effective ways to reduce the spread of the disease include maintaining household hygiene, frequently cleaning and disinfecting frequently touched surfaces, practising good hand hygiene, and ensuring proper indoor air circulation. Members of the public may refer to the CHP'sCOVID-19 & Flu Express,Seasonal Influenza WebpageandVaccination Schemesfor the latest information. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

德国2024–2026年A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒中NA-S247N替换持续增加及其奥司他韦敏感性降低的分子与表型监测研究 - 生物通

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报A German study published on 4 October 2026 documents a dramatic rise in the neuraminidase substitution NA‐S247N among influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses, from 1.16 % in the 2024/25 season to 56.8 % in 2025/26. The mutation confers a modest reduction in oseltamivir susceptibility, with a four‐fold increase in IC50 values. The research, conducted by the National Influenza Center in Berlin using nationwide sentinel surveillance, analyzed 776 virus isolates and employed RT‐PCR, pyrosequencing and cell‐culture inhibition assays. While no human cases or fatalities were linked to this mutation, the findings highlight a growing reservoir of oseltamivir‐resistant viruses that could constrain treatment options. WHO guidance calls for continued surveillance and consideration of alternative antivirals when resistance is detected. The study reinforces the importance of rapid, routine molecular and phenotypic monitoring to detect emerging antiviral resistance early.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-10-04;研究期间:2024/25和2025/26流感季节(2024年10月至2026年3月)。
地点与对象
德国 – 国家流感中心(柏林罗伯特·科赫研究所);全国急性呼吸道感染哨点监测;从全国收集的6878(2024/25)和4910(2025/26)呼吸道样本中分离出776株甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病毒。
事件
一项报告流感甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09病毒中神经氨酸酶替代NA-S247N出现显著增加的研究,并指出其与奥司他韦抗性略有降低相关。该研究已被确认并发表于《国际医学微生物学杂志》。
规模、影响与风险
NA‐S247N prevalence rose from 1.16 % in 2024/25 to 56.8 % in 2025/26; viruses with this mutation showed a ~4‐fold increase in oseltamivir IC50. No human cases or deaths were reported; the finding indicates a growing population of viruses with reduced antiviral sensitivity, potentially limiting treatment options.
应对、状态与不确定性
德国国家流感中心继续进行常规的分子和表型监测,采用RT-PCR、焦磷酸测序和细胞培养抑制试验。世界卫生组织建议持续监测抗病毒药物抗性,并在检测到抗性时建议使用替代抗病毒药物(例如巴洛沙韦)。该研究强调了快速筛查和持续监测的必要性,但未报告任何立即的公共卫生干预措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
德国2024–2026年A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒中NA-S247N替换持续增加及其奥司他韦敏感性降低的分子与表型监测研究 - 生物通
News Brief
A German study published on 4 October 2026 documents a dramatic rise in the neuraminidase substitution NA‐S247N among influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses, from 1.16 % in the 2024/25 season to 56.8 % in 2025/26. The mutation confers a modest reduction in oseltamivir susceptibility, with a four‐fold increase in IC50 values. The research, conducted by the National Influenza Center in Berlin using nationwide sentinel surveillance, analyzed 776 virus isolates and employed RT‐PCR, pyrosequencing and cell‐culture inhibition assays. While no human cases or fatalities were linked to this mutation, the findings highlight a growing reservoir of oseltamivir‐resistant viruses that could constrain treatment options. WHO guidance calls for continued surveillance and consideration of alternative antivirals when resistance is detected. The study reinforces the importance of rapid, routine molecular and phenotypic monitoring to detect emerging antiviral resistance early.
Original source text (Chinese)
德国2024–2026年A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒中NA-S247N替换持续增加及其奥司他韦敏感性降低的分子与表型监测研究 《International Journal of Medical Microbiology》:Increased circulation of influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses with reduced susceptibility to oseltamivir, Germany 2024–2026 【字体: 大 中 小 】 时间:2026年10月04日 来源:International Journal of Medical Microbiology 3.2 编辑推荐: 德国2024–2026年对奥司他韦(oseltamivir)敏感性降低的A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒传播增加 •德国国家流感中心(National Influenza Center for Germany, Germany NIC)检测到携带神经氨酸酶(n 下载钙粘蛋白海报药物开发的最新进展。钙粘蛋白(Cadherins)是癌症、心血管等疾病的治疗靶点。 为了促进科学的传播,生物通采集了这篇文章 订阅BioHot点这里解锁原文链接推送! “畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Open access 德国2024–2026年对奥司他韦(oseltamivir)敏感性降低的A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒传播增加 •德国国家流感中心(National Influenza Center for Germany, Germany NIC)检测到携带神经氨酸酶(neuraminidase, NA)替换NA-S247N的A(H1N1)pdm09病毒流行率增加,从2024/25流感季循环的1.16%上升至2025/26流感季的56.8%。•NA-S247N替换轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性;联合NA-S247N/NA-I223V/T替换按世界卫生组织(World Health Organization, WHO)标准显示抑制降低。•持续分子和表型监测对公共卫生以及早期发现对抗病毒药物敏感性降低的病毒至关重要。•快速焦磷酸测序(pyrosequencing)支持对与抗病毒药物敏感性降低相关的神经氨酸酶替换进行常规监测。•研究结果体现德国NIC在抗病毒敏感性监测方面30年的贡献。 论文主体解读 免费下载【类器官培养与鉴定指南】,帮您快速选择不同类器官培养所需的细胞因子和标志物抗体。 领 取 研究背景方面,流感是高度传染性呼吸道病毒感染,主要通过咳嗽和喷嚏产生的气溶胶飞沫传播,全球每年估计造成300万–500万例重症和290,000–650,000例死亡。在德国,甲型和乙型流感病毒每个冬季通常感染5–10%成人和20–30%儿童。接种疫苗仍是最有效预防措施;当接种不可行或对脆弱人群效果有限时,抗病毒预防和治疗提供必要替代。WHO有条件推荐奥司他韦作为神经氨酸酶抑制剂(neuraminidase inhibitor, NAI)一线抗病毒药,用于症状出现48小时内严重季节性流感。对奥司他韦敏感性降低在循环病毒中全球流行率低于1%,但尤其在免疫抑制患者中可经治疗选择出现。自2023/24冬季以来,德国、欧盟/欧洲经济区部分国家及部分WHO区域出现携带NA-I223V和/或NA-S247N的A(H1N1)pdm09病毒,在缺乏其他NA分子耐药标记时轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性。携带降低抗病毒抑制突变的病毒可保持适应性和传播性,因此其循环增加对流感管理构成挑战。WHO要求国家流感中心(National Influenza Center, NIC)定期开展抗病毒敏感性检测,结合表型试验与PA、NA、M2基因分子耐药标记鉴定。位于柏林的Robert Koch Institute的NIC自2006年以来持续及时检测,1996–2006年病毒为回顾性检测,并参与WHO全球流感监测与应对系统。本文报告2024/25和2025/26流感季德国A(H1N1)pdm09病毒对奥司他韦敏感性降低的增加,由NA-I223V和NA-S247N出现驱动。 研究人员通过德国哨点监测、基因型与表型分析,追踪A(H1N1)pdm09病毒中NA替换及奥司他韦和扎那米韦敏感性,结论是NA-S247N从2024/25季的1.16%升至2025/26季的56.8%;单独NA-S247N轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性,与NA-I223V/T联合时按WHO标准出现抑制降低。其意义在于强调持续分子和表型监测、快速PSQ筛查对公共卫生和早期发现敏感性降低病毒的重要性,并体现德国NIC 30年抗病毒敏感性监测贡献。 关键技术方法概括(不超过250字):作者Susanne C. Duwe、Mohammad Naneh、Jeanette Milde、Ralf Dürrwald利用德国全国急性呼吸道感染(acute respiratory illness, ARI)哨点监测,由德国流感工作组运行,样本为鼻、喉或咽拭子;2024/25季收集6878份、2025/26季收集4910份样本,分析776株A(H1N1)pdm09病毒。主要技术包括逆转录PCR(reverse transcription PCR, RT-PCR)分型/亚型、下一代测序(next-generation sequencing, NGS)及GISAID/FluSurver/WHO位点分析、针对NA-I223V和NA-S247N的RT-PCR后焦磷酸测序(pyrosequencing, PSQ)、NA基因系统发育分析、MDCK-SIAT1细胞病毒分离和神经氨酸酶抑制试验测定奥司他韦与扎那米韦半数抑制浓度(50% inhibitory concentration, IC 50 )。样本队列来源为德国全国ARI哨点监测。 研究结果如下。 4.1 设计与验证用于快速检测A(H1N1)pdm09 NA-I223V和NA-S247N替换的RT-PCR与PSQ试验:研究人员通过比对代表性NA序列与参考株A/California/7/2009,设计扩增181-bp NA基因片段的RT-PCR引物,覆盖氨基酸位置223和247,并使用生物素化反义引物和测序引物。负对照均阴性,PCR条件优化后扩增产物为单一特异条带。与NGS生成的全长NA序列平行验证44个NA基因,PSQ可清楚区分野生型和突变密码子:NA-I223V野生型ATA(异亮氨酸)与突变GTA(缬氨酸),NA-S247N野生型AGT(丝氨酸)与突变AAT(天冬酰胺)。结论是PSQ可特异检测这两个替换,无需运行后分析。 4.2 与神经氨酸酶抑制剂敏感性降低相关的A(H1N1)pdm09病毒循环:2024年10月至2025年4月共收集6878份样本,27%为流感病毒阳性;2024/25季A(H1N1)pdm09和乙型流感为主,分别45.6%和44.0%。2025/26季4910份样本中22.6%阳性,以A(H3N2)为主,占70.2%,A(H1N1)pdm09占29.6%。两季共分析776株A(H1N1)pdm09,2024/25季62%、2025/26季79%通过表型、基因型或两者表征。2024/25季检测到2例NA-H275Y,对奥司他韦高度降低敏感性,对扎那米韦仍敏感;8例携带NA-S247N,其中5例在2025年2月季节高峰;1例携带NA-I223T。2025/26季NA-S247N流行率自2025年10月持续上升,3月达分析病毒的80%,总体从1.16%升至56.8%。另有两株同时携带NA-S247N与223位替换(分别为NA-I223V和NA-I223T)。未检测到其他与敏感性降低或耐药相关替换。系统发育分析显示,携带NA-S247N并表现奥司他韦敏感性降低的病毒按季节和分支聚集,2023/24季为C5.3亚分支,2024/25季为D2亚分支,2025/26季与奥司他韦敏感当代株共同位于D3亚分支,未形成独立谱系。 4.3 循环A(H1N1)pdm09病毒对神经氨酸酶抑制剂的表型敏感性:两季共350株A(H1N1)pdm09经细胞培养并测定奥司他韦和扎那米韦IC 50 ,分别占2024/25季检出A(H1N1)pdm09的20%和2025/26季的56%。排除高度降低敏感性的病毒后,扎那米韦平均IC 50 从2024/25季0.9±0.4 nM升至2025/26季2.1±1.2 nM;奥司他韦从1.1±0.6 nM升至4.6±2.7 nM,约4倍增加。月度中位数从2025年11月至2026年3月持续增加。与野生型相比,携带NA-S247N的病毒奥司他韦耐药因子(resistance factor, RF)约增加4倍,扎那米韦仅1.6倍(N=133)。两株同时携带NA-S247N和NA-I223V/T的病毒奥司他韦RF最高增加17倍,扎那米韦RF增加4倍。结论是NA-S247N轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性,双突变病毒按WHO标准表现为抑制降低。 讨论部分总结:自NA-V241I和NA-N369K在2010/11季成为A(H1N1)pdm09稳定谱系后,其他改变NA抗病毒谱的替换仅零星出现。允许性变化可能促进NA-H275Y等降低敏感性标记出现;NA-H275Y高度降低奥司他韦敏感性,破坏275位残基周围氢键网络和相邻250-loop,阻止E276旋转及形成奥司他韦结合口袋所需的E276-R224盐桥,从而降低奥司他韦亲和力,同时保留扎那米韦和天然唾液酸受体结合。双突变病毒奥司他韦IC 50 显著增加,说明协同和潜在允许性替换可放大抗病毒敏感性降低。NA-I223V轻微降低酶亲和力,NA-S247N改变活性位点氢键,可能降低奥司他韦敏感性;两者均可能影响NA-H275Y诱导的高度降低敏感性。NA替换逐渐积累可降低抗病毒敏感性,并可能限制治疗选择。NA-I223V/R曾在葡萄牙、伊朗及儿童长期奥司他韦治疗后社区样本中零星检测;德国2015/16和2023/24季曾短暂上升后几乎下降。当前NA-S247N持续增加,提示其可能变得更常见。2023/24季双突变病毒在荷兰、法国、孟加拉国、阿曼、英国等检测,香港、尼日尔、澳大利亚、西班牙、美国较少,加拿大、埃塞俄比亚、马尔代夫、挪威、瑞典各1例;这些病毒对奥司他韦抑制降低13倍,对其他抗病毒仍正常敏感。全球监测显示NA-I223V在2023年8月和10月达峰,NA-S247N在2023年9月达峰;西班牙基于GISAID数据报道2025年7–10月加泰罗尼亚和其他EU/EEA区域仅携带NA-S247N的A(H1N1)pdm09周流行率在20%–100%波动。德国数据表明2025/26季该模式加强。系统发育显示携带NA-S247N且奥司他韦敏感性降低的病毒按季节和分支聚集,无独立谱系,与近期结果一致。国际检测强调持续全球监测的重要性,包括日本等抗病毒使用和治疗实践不同的地区。该敏感性降低的临床意义仍不确定,但可能影响治疗决策;在临床适宜并符合当地指南时可考虑替代抗病毒药如baloxavir marboxil。协调流感预防策略,尤其针对学龄儿童的同步疫苗接种,可减少跨年龄传播并促进人群层面流感抑制。降低抗病毒敏感性仍是持续关注问题;按WHO建议,德国NIC持续开展循环流感病毒分子和表型监测,以识别新发耐药模式。观察到的A(H1N1)pdm09抗病毒敏感性变化强调德国NIC过去30年对流感和循环病毒特征描述的持续贡献。结合表型检测与靶向基因型分析有助于发现流感病毒抗病毒敏感性的早期变化;RT-PCR后PSQ支持高通量流程,仅需短扩增子和预定位点靶向测序,相对全基因测序减少周转时间和成本;与终点基因型方法相比,PSQ可在与敏感性降低相关密码子处提供逐碱基确认。因此,RT-PCR/PSQ试验为常规监测和特定替换早期发现提供快速实用工具。 研究结论翻译:总之,本研究表明,NA替换的积累,特别是A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒中NA-S247N与NA-I223V/T联合,可显著降低奥司他韦敏感性。2025/26季德国NA-S247N流行率显著增加,与全球其他区域发现一致,提示该替换正变得更持续而非短暂。这突出流感病毒抗病毒敏感性的动态性质,并强调持续分子和表型监测的重要性。将表型检测与靶向基因型分析(包括基于PSQ的筛查)整合,能够早期发现新发耐药模式,并支持流感治疗和公共卫生应对的循证决策。 论文主体解读研究背景方面,流感是高度传染性呼吸道病毒感染,主要通过咳嗽和喷嚏产生的气溶胶飞沫传播,全球每年估计造成300万–500万例重症和290,000–650,000例死亡。在德国,甲型和乙型流感病毒每个冬季通常感染5–10%成人和20–30%儿童。接种疫苗仍是最有效预防措施;当接种不可行或对脆弱人群效果有限时,抗病毒预防和治疗提供必要替代。WHO有条件推荐奥司他韦作为神经氨酸酶抑制剂(neuraminidase inhibitor, NAI)一线抗病毒药,用于症状出现48小时内严重季节性流感。对奥司他韦敏感性降低在循环病毒中全球流行率低于1%,但尤其在免疫抑制患者中可经治疗选择出现。自2023/24冬季以来,德国、欧盟/欧洲经济区部分国家及部分WHO区域出现携带NA-I223V和/或NA-S247N的A(H1N1)pdm09病毒,在缺乏其他NA分子耐药标记时轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性。携带降低抗病毒抑制突变的病毒可保持适应性和传播性,因此其循环增加对流感管理构成挑战。WHO要求国家流感中心(National Influenza Center, NIC)定期开展抗病毒敏感性检测,结合表型试验与PA、NA、M2基因分子耐药标记鉴定。位于柏林的Robert Koch Institute的NIC自2006年以来持续及时检测,1996–2006年病毒为回顾性检测,并参与WHO全球流感监测与应对系统。本文报告2024/25和2025/26流感季德国A(H1N1)pdm09病毒对奥司他韦敏感性降低的增加,由NA-I223V和NA-S247N出现驱动。研究人员通过德国哨点监测、基因型与表型分析,追踪A(H1N1)pdm09病毒中NA替换及奥司他韦和扎那米韦敏感性,结论是NA-S247N从2024/25季的1.16%升至2025/26季的56.8%;单独NA-S247N轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性,与NA-I223V/T联合时按WHO标准出现抑制降低。其意义在于强调持续分子和表型监测、快速PSQ筛查对公共卫生和早期发现敏感性降低病毒的重要性,并体现德国NIC 30年抗病毒敏感性监测贡献。关键技术方法概括(不超过250字):作者Susanne C. Duwe、Mohammad Naneh、Jeanette Milde、Ralf Dürrwald利用德国全国急性呼吸道感染(acute respiratory illness, ARI)哨点监测,由德国流感工作组运行,样本为鼻、喉或咽拭子;2024/25季收集6878份、2025/26季收集4910份样本,分析776株A(H1N1)pdm09病毒。主要技术包括逆转录PCR(reverse transcription PCR, RT-PCR)分型/亚型、下一代测序(next-generation sequencing, NGS)及GISAID/FluSurver/WHO位点分析、针对NA-I223V和NA-S247N的RT-PCR后焦磷酸测序(pyrosequencing, PSQ)、NA基因系统发育分析、MDCK-SIAT1细胞病毒分离和神经氨酸酶抑制试验测定奥司他韦与扎那米韦半数抑制浓度(50% inhibitory concentration, IC)。样本队列来源为德国全国ARI哨点监测。研究结果如下。4.1 设计与验证用于快速检测A(H1N1)pdm09 NA-I223V和NA-S247N替换的RT-PCR与PSQ试验:研究人员通过比对代表性NA序列与参考株A/California/7/2009,设计扩增181-bp NA基因片段的RT-PCR引物,覆盖氨基酸位置223和247,并使用生物素化反义引物和测序引物。负对照均阴性,PCR条件优化后扩增产物为单一特异条带。与NGS生成的全长NA序列平行验证44个NA基因,PSQ可清楚区分野生型和突变密码子:NA-I223V野生型ATA(异亮氨酸)与突变GTA(缬氨酸),NA-S247N野生型AGT(丝氨酸)与突变AAT(天冬酰胺)。结论是PSQ可特异检测这两个替换,无需运行后分析。4.2 与神经氨酸酶抑制剂敏感性降低相关的A(H1N1)pdm09病毒循环:2024年10月至2025年4月共收集6878份样本,27%为流感病毒阳性;2024/25季A(H1N1)pdm09和乙型流感为主,分别45.6%和44.0%。2025/26季4910份样本中22.6%阳性,以A(H3N2)为主,占70.2%,A(H1N1)pdm09占29.6%。两季共分析776株A(H1N1)pdm09,2024/25季62%、2025/26季79%通过表型、基因型或两者表征。2024/25季检测到2例NA-H275Y,对奥司他韦高度降低敏感性,对扎那米韦仍敏感;8例携带NA-S247N,其中5例在2025年2月季节高峰;1例携带NA-I223T。2025/26季NA-S247N流行率自2025年10月持续上升,3月达分析病毒的80%,总体从1.16%升至56.8%。另有两株同时携带NA-S247N与223位替换(分别为NA-I223V和NA-I223T)。未检测到其他与敏感性降低或耐药相关替换。系统发育分析显示,携带NA-S247N并表现奥司他韦敏感性降低的病毒按季节和分支聚集,2023/24季为C5.3亚分支,2024/25季为D2亚分支,2025/26季与奥司他韦敏感当代株共同位于D3亚分支,未形成独立谱系。4.3 循环A(H1N1)pdm09病毒对神经氨酸酶抑制剂的表型敏感性:两季共350株A(H1N1)pdm09经细胞培养并测定奥司他韦和扎那米韦IC,分别占2024/25季检出A(H1N1)pdm09的20%和2025/26季的56%。排除高度降低敏感性的病毒后,扎那米韦平均IC从2024/25季0.9±0.4 nM升至2025/26季2.1±1.2 nM;奥司他韦从1.1±0.6 nM升至4.6±2.7 nM,约4倍增加。月度中位数从2025年11月至2026年3月持续增加。与野生型相比,携带NA-S247N的病毒奥司他韦耐药因子(resistance factor, RF)约增加4倍,扎那米韦仅1.6倍(N=133)。两株同时携带NA-S247N和NA-I223V/T的病毒奥司他韦RF最高增加17倍,扎那米韦RF增加4倍。结论是NA-S247N轻度降低奥司他韦敏感性,双突变病毒按WHO标准表现为抑制降低。讨论部分总结:自NA-V241I和NA-N369K在2010/11季成为A(H1N1)pdm09稳定谱系后,其他改变NA抗病毒谱的替换仅零星出现。允许性变化可能促进NA-H275Y等降低敏感性标记出现;NA-H275Y高度降低奥司他韦敏感性,破坏275位残基周围氢键网络和相邻250-loop,阻止E276旋转及形成奥司他韦结合口袋所需的E276-R224盐桥,从而降低奥司他韦亲和力,同时保留扎那米韦和天然唾液酸受体结合。双突变病毒奥司他韦IC显著增加,说明协同和潜在允许性替换可放大抗病毒敏感性降低。NA-I223V轻微降低酶亲和力,NA-S247N改变活性位点氢键,可能降低奥司他韦敏感性;两者均可能影响NA-H275Y诱导的高度降低敏感性。NA替换逐渐积累可降低抗病毒敏感性,并可能限制治疗选择。NA-I223V/R曾在葡萄牙、伊朗及儿童长期奥司他韦治疗后社区样本中零星检测;德国2015/16和2023/24季曾短暂上升后几乎下降。当前NA-S247N持续增加,提示其可能变得更常见。2023/24季双突变病毒在荷兰、法国、孟加拉国、阿曼、英国等检测,香港、尼日尔、澳大利亚、西班牙、美国较少,加拿大、埃塞俄比亚、马尔代夫、挪威、瑞典各1例;这些病毒对奥司他韦抑制降低13倍,对其他抗病毒仍正常敏感。全球监测显示NA-I223V在2023年8月和10月达峰,NA-S247N在2023年9月达峰;西班牙基于GISAID数据报道2025年7–10月加泰罗尼亚和其他EU/EEA区域仅携带NA-S247N的A(H1N1)pdm09周流行率在20%–100%波动。德国数据表明2025/26季该模式加强。系统发育显示携带NA-S247N且奥司他韦敏感性降低的病毒按季节和分支聚集,无独立谱系,与近期结果一致。国际检测强调持续全球监测的重要性,包括日本等抗病毒使用和治疗实践不同的地区。该敏感性降低的临床意义仍不确定,但可能影响治疗决策;在临床适宜并符合当地指南时可考虑替代抗病毒药如baloxavir marboxil。协调流感预防策略,尤其针对学龄儿童的同步疫苗接种,可减少跨年龄传播并促进人群层面流感抑制。降低抗病毒敏感性仍是持续关注问题;按WHO建议,德国NIC持续开展循环流感病毒分子和表型监测,以识别新发耐药模式。观察到的A(H1N1)pdm09抗病毒敏感性变化强调德国NIC过去30年对流感和循环病毒特征描述的持续贡献。结合表型检测与靶向基因型分析有助于发现流感病毒抗病毒敏感性的早期变化;RT-PCR后PSQ支持高通量流程,仅需短扩增子和预定位点靶向测序,相对全基因测序减少周转时间和成本;与终点基因型方法相比,PSQ可在与敏感性降低相关密码子处提供逐碱基确认。因此,RT-PCR/PSQ试验为常规监测和特定替换早期发现提供快速实用工具。研究结论翻译:总之,本研究表明,NA替换的积累,特别是A(H1N1)pdm09流感病毒中NA-S247N与NA-I223V/T联合,可显著降低奥司他韦敏感性。2025/26季德国NA-S247N流行率显著增加,与全球其他区域发现一致,提示该替换正变得更持续而非短暂。这突出流感病毒抗病毒敏感性的动态性质,并强调持续分子和表型监测的重要性。将表型检测与靶向基因型分析(包括基于PSQ的筛查)整合,能够早期发现新发耐药模式,并支持流感治疗和公共卫生应对的循证决策。 View five news elements
Time
Publication date: 2026-10-04; Study period: 2024/25 and 2025/26 influenza seasons (October 2024–March 2026).
Location and population
Germany – National Influenza Center (Robert Koch Institute, Berlin); national acute respiratory infection sentinel surveillance; 776 A(H1N1)pdm09 virus isolates from 6878 (2024/25) and 4910 (2025/26) respiratory samples collected nationwide.
Event
A research study reporting a marked increase in the prevalence of the neuraminidase substitution NA‐S247N in influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses and its associated modest reduction in oseltamivir susceptibility. The study is confirmed and published in the International Journal of Medical Microbiology.
Scale, impact and risk
NA‐S247N prevalence rose from 1.16 % in 2024/25 to 56.8 % in 2025/26; viruses with this mutation showed a ~4‐fold increase in oseltamivir IC50. No human cases or deaths were reported; the finding indicates a growing population of viruses with reduced antiviral sensitivity, potentially limiting treatment options.
Response, status and uncertainty
Germany’s National Influenza Center continues routine molecular and phenotypic surveillance, employing RT‐PCR, pyrosequencing and cell‐culture inhibition assays. WHO recommends ongoing monitoring of antiviral resistance and suggests alternative antivirals (e.g., baloxavir) when resistance is detected. The study underscores the need for rapid screening and sustained surveillance but does not report any immediate public‐health interventions.
原文 / Source

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

[特辑] 专家解读:为什么流感正在传播?可能包括'海外旅行'和'免疫力下降'等多重因素

[Special Feature] Experts Explain: Why is Influenza Spreading? Multiple Factors Likely Including 'Overseas Travel' and 'Lowered Immunity'
新闻简报Seasonal influenza is spreading in Ishikawa and Toyama, Japan, earlier than usual, according to a report by Professor Hiroshi Ichimura of Kanazawa University. The outbreak is attributed to multiple factors, including the influx of viruses from overseas due to increased travel, lowered immunity following the reduced circulation of influenza during the COVID-19 pandemic, environmental conditions in summer such as dryness and poor ventilation, and increased human mobility. While there were concerns about the emergence of a new virus or variant, none have been confirmed. The outbreak is expected to peak in winter, with Type A influenza likely peaking between November and December, and Type B between January and March of next year. Public health measures include hand washing, mask-wearing, humidity control, and ensuring proper ventilation. The article provides general background and prevention guidance, with no confirmed cases or specific risk levels reported.
查看新闻五要素
时间
流感暴发被描述为比往常早得多开始传播,当前时间为2026年10月(N15)。预计传播将在冬季达到高峰,其中甲型流感可能在11月至12月达到高峰,乙型流感可能在明年1月至3月达到高峰(N12)。
地点与对象
暴发在日本石川县和富山县被报告(N2)。受影响的人群包括因学校重新开放而重新聚集的儿童,以及因免疫力下降而受影响的一般人群(N9)。
事件
季节性流感在日本石川县和富山县传播,因素包括海外旅行、免疫力下降、环境条件和人类活动增加等(N4)。该事件由金泽大学教授Ichimura Hiroshi报告(N3)。
规模、影响与风险
暴发在日本石川县和富山县传播,未确认新病毒或变种的病例(N11)。风险被认为较低,不会迅速升级,冬季高峰预计如常出现(N12)。
应对、状态与不确定性
应对措施包括洗手、佩戴口罩、控制湿度和保持窗户开启以通风(N16)。文章是对暴发背景和预防措施的一般解释(N14)。

[Special Feature] Experts Explain: Why is Influenza Spreading? Multiple Factors Likely Including 'Overseas Travel' and 'Lowered Immunity'

News Brief
Seasonal influenza is spreading in Ishikawa and Toyama, Japan, earlier than usual, according to a report by Professor Hiroshi Ichimura of Kanazawa University. The outbreak is attributed to multiple factors, including the influx of viruses from overseas due to increased travel, lowered immunity following the reduced circulation of influenza during the COVID-19 pandemic, environmental conditions in summer such as dryness and poor ventilation, and increased human mobility. While there were concerns about the emergence of a new virus or variant, none have been confirmed. The outbreak is expected to peak in winter, with Type A influenza likely peaking between November and December, and Type B between January and March of next year. Public health measures include hand washing, mask-wearing, humidity control, and ensuring proper ventilation. The article provides general background and prevention guidance, with no confirmed cases or specific risk levels reported.
Original source text (Japanese)
メインコンテンツへスキップ [Special Feature] Experts Explain: Why is Influenza Spreading? Multiple Factors Likely Including 'Overseas Travel' and 'Lowered Immunity' ゆうと 金沢キッチンカー放浪記|ガイドブックに載らない街の日常と味 2026年10月4日 17:56 Seasonal influenza is spreading in Ishikawa and Toyama. We asked Professor Emeritus Hiroshi Ichimura of Kanazawa University (specializing in viral infectious disease control) about the background of this outbreak. [Illustration] Key points for preventing infectious diseases The background to the influenza outbreak starting much earlier than usual is suggested to be a combination of multiple factors, including (1) the influx of viruses from overseas, (2) lowered immunity, (3) environmental factors specific to summer, and (4) increased human mobility. During the COVID-19 pandemic from 2020 to 2022, influenza barely circulated, leading to a decline in the population's overall immunity to the virus. Viruses that were circulating in Southern Hemisphere countries were brought in early by an increase in overseas travelers, driven by factors like the weak yen. Furthermore, it is believed that the increase in human movement during summer vacation caused the infection to spread. In summer, people tend to keep windows closed and use air conditioning. The resulting dryness and lack of ventilation, along with increased contact among children due to schools reopening, are likely contributing factors to the outbreak. In 2009, when a new virus was circulating, the outbreak also began earlier than usual, entering the epidemic phase in September after summer vacation. There were concerns about the emergence of a new virus or a new variant this season as well, but none have been confirmed to date. It is unlikely that the summer outbreak will escalate rapidly, and it is expected that the winter peak will arrive as usual, with Type A peaking between November and December, and Type B between January and March of next year. Vaccines provide sufficient antibody production about two weeks after inoculation, and the duration of effectiveness is considered to be three to five months. While elderly people and high-risk patients may want to consider early vaccination, they should not overexert themselves and should prepare for the upcoming winter peak. It is now October, and the heat has subsided. In addition to hand washing, wearing masks, and humidity control, please try to keep windows open and ensure adequate ventilation in facilities and other areas. * The article and images for this feature were created without the use of AI. #インフルエンザ #ウイルス #学校再開 #海外旅行者 #持続期間 #換気不足 #流行入り #インフルエンザ #ウイルス #学校再開 #海外旅行者 #持続期間 #換気不足 #流行入り トップ 健康・医療 病気 あなたへのおすすめ ピックアップされています 私の世界を、少し広げてくれたnote 6,666本

Time
The influenza outbreak is described as starting much earlier than usual, with the current time being October 2026 (N15). The spread is expected to peak in winter, with Type A between November and December, and Type B between January and March of next year (N12).
Location and population
The outbreak is reported in Ishikawa and Toyama, Japan (N2). The affected population includes children due to schools reopening, and the general population with lowered immunity (N9).
Event
Seasonal influenza is spreading in Ishikawa and Toyama, Japan, with factors such as overseas travel, lowered immunity, environmental conditions, and increased human mobility contributing to the spread (N4). The event is reported by Professor Hiroshi Ichimura of Kanazawa University (N3).
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak is described as spreading in Ishikawa and Toyama, with no confirmed cases of new viruses or variants (N11). The risk is considered low for a rapid escalation, with the winter peak expected as usual (N12).
Response, status and uncertainty
The response includes recommendations for hand washing, mask-wearing, humidity control, and keeping windows open for ventilation (N16). The article is a general explanation of the outbreak's background and prevention measures (N14).
